   459	    12	/**
   460	    13	 * `baton detect` — classify harness output / structured error types.
   461	    14	 * Exit codes ARE the verdict (frozen contract): 0 ok · 10 usage-limit ·
   462	    15	 * 11 throttle · 12 auth · 13 other-error · 2 usage error.
   463	    16	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
   464	    17	 * @returns {number}
   465	    18	 */
   466	    19	export function cmdDetect(args, io) {
   467	    20	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { platform: 'string', text: 'string', 'exit-code': 'string', 'structured-error-type': 'string', signatures: 'string', explain: 'boolean' });
   468	    21	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'detect', parsed.error);
   469	    22	  const flags = parsed.flags;
   470	    23	  const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
   471	    24	  if (!platform) return usageError(io, flags, 'detect', '--platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required');
   472	    25

   476	    29	      {

   478	    31	        overlayPath: typeof flags.signatures === 'string' ? flags.signatures : undefined,

   491	    44

   493	    46	  let structured = null;

   499	    52	  }

   516	     1	/**
   517	     2	 * Pure, explainable role resolution over the committed matrix. Every decision
   518	     3	 * is auditable: chosen entries carry their chainIndex; passed-over entries land
   519	     4	 * in skipped[] with a why. avoid[] population is the caller's job.
   520	     5	 * @param {{config: any, to: string, avoid?: string[], nativeOnly?: boolean, probes?: Record<string, {capability: string, outcome: string}> | null}} input
   521	     6	 * @returns {{assignments: Record<string, any>, notes: string[]}}
   522	     7	 */
   523	     8	export function resolveRoles({ config, to, avoid = [], nativeOnly = false, probes = null }) {
   524	     9	  /** @type {string[]} */
   525	    10	  const notes = [];
   526	    11	
   527	    12	  /**
   528	    13	   * The eligibility verdict for a platform, or null when eligible.
   529	    14	   * @param {string} platform @returns {string | null}
   530	    15	   */
   531	    16	  function skipReason(platform) {
   532	    17	    if (avoid.includes(platform)) return 'avoided';
   533	    18	    if (!(platform in (config.platforms ?? {}))) return 'unknown-platform';
   534	    19	    if (config.platforms[platform]?.enabled === false) return 'disabled';
   535	    20	    if (probes && probes[platform]) {
   536	    21	      const { capability, outcome } = probes[platform];
   537	    22	      if (outcome === 'rate-limited') return 'rate-limited';
   538	    23	      if (capability === 'not-installed') return 'not-installed';
   539	    24	      // 'installed' means installation was PROVEN but the probe was capped there
   540	    25	      // (capOnSuccess) — authentication is UNVERIFIED, not verified-failed. Only
   541	    26	      // a probe that ran and did NOT succeed (outcome !== 'ok') is a verified
   542	    27	      // auth/reachability failure worth skipping; a capped success stays
   543	    28	      // selectable and is flagged degraded below (iter-3 F1, plan §Role matrix).
   544	    29	      if (capability === 'installed' && outcome !== 'ok') return 'unauthenticated';
   545	    30	    }
   546	    31	    return null;
   547	    32	  }
   548	    33	
   549	    34	  // A selection is degraded when its availability is UNVERIFIED (gate-2 iter-2
   550	    35	  // M3, iter-3 F1): with no probe cache at all, resolution is offline and EVERY
   551	    36	  // selection is degraded (plan: "Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged
   552	    37	  // degraded"); with a partial cache, platforms the cache is missing are; and a
   553	    38	  // platform probed only to 'installed' (auth unverified, e.g. a --version
   554	    39	  // success) is degraded even though it stays selectable.
   555	    40	  /** @param {string} platform */
   556	    41	  const probeUnverified = (platform) => {
   557	    42	    if (probes == null || !probes[platform]) return true;
   558	    43	    const { capability, outcome } = probes[platform];
   559	    44	    // Degraded when ANY required dimension is unverified: a capped 'installed'
   560	    45	    // (auth never confirmed) OR a non-ok outcome (iter-4 I6) — a selectable
   561	    46	    // platform whose last probe ERRORED or TIMED OUT is presented as
   562	    47	    // reachability-verified otherwise, which is exactly the silent-degradation
   563	    48	    // the plan's degraded flag exists to surface. (rate-limited is skipped, not
   564	    49	    // selected, so it never reaches here.)
   565	    50	    return capability === 'installed' || outcome !== 'ok';
   566	    51	  };
   567	    52	
   568	    53	  /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
   569	    54	  const assignments = {};
   570	    55	  for (const [role, chain] of Object.entries(config.roles)) {
   571	    56	    /** @type {any[]} */
   572	    57	    const skipped = [];
   573	    58	    /** @type {any} */
   574	    59	    let assignment = null;
   575	    60	
   576	    61	    for (let i = 0; i < chain.length; i += 1) {
   577	    62	      const entry = chain[i];
   578	    63	      if (nativeOnly && entry.platform !== to) {
   579	    64	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why: 'non-native' });
   580	    65	        continue;
   581	    66	      }
   582	    67	      const why = skipReason(entry.platform);
   583	    68	      if (why) {
   584	    69	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why });
   585	    70	        continue;
   586	    71	      }
   587	    72	      assignment = {
   588	    73	        platform: entry.platform,
   589	    74	        model: entry.model,
   590	    75	        effort: entry.effort ?? null,
   591	    76	        mode: entry.platform === to ? 'native' : 'delegated',
   592	    77	        chainIndex: i,
   593	    78	        skipped,
   594	    79	      };
   595	    80	      if (probeUnverified(entry.platform)) assignment.degraded = true;
   596	    81	      break;
   597	    82	    }
   598	    83	
   599	    84	    if (!assignment && nativeOnly) {
   600	    85	      // Forced-default is only valid when the destination itself is eligible.
   601	    86	      const whyTo = skipReason(to);
   602	    87	      const model = config.defaults?.[to];
   603	    88	      if (!whyTo && typeof model === 'string') {
   604	    89	        assignment = { platform: to, model, effort: null, mode: 'forced-default', chainIndex: null, skipped };
   605	    90	        if (probeUnverified(to)) assignment.degraded = true;
   606	    91	      }
   607	    92	    }
   608	    93	
   609	    94	    if (!assignment) {
   610	    95	      assignment = { platform: null, model: null, effort: null, mode: 'unavailable', chainIndex: null, skipped };
   611	    96	    }
   612	    97	    assignments[role] = assignment;
   613	    98	  }
   614	    99	
   615	   100	  return { assignments, notes };
   616	   101	}

   621	     1	const PRECEDENCE = ['usage-limit', 'auth', 'throttle', 'other-error'];
   622	     2	
   623	     3	/** @type {Record<string, string>} */
   624	     4	const STOP_FAILURE_MAP = {
   625	     5	  rate_limit: 'usage-limit',
   626	     6	  overloaded: 'throttle',
   627	     7	  authentication_failed: 'auth',
   628	     8	  oauth_org_not_allowed: 'auth',
   629	     9	  billing_error: 'auth',
   630	    10	};
   631	    11	
   632	    12	// eslint-disable-next-line no-control-regex
   633	    13	const ANSI = /\x1b\[[0-9;]*[A-Za-z]/g;
   634	    14	
   635	    15	/** @param {any} matcher @param {string} text */
   636	    16	function matches(matcher, text) {
   637	    17	  switch (matcher.kind) {
   638	    18	    case 'substring':
   639	    19	      return text.includes(matcher.value);
   640	    20	    case 'regex':
   641	    21	      return new RegExp(matcher.pattern, matcher.flags || '').test(text);
   642	    22	    case 'json-field': {
   643	    23	      for (const line of text.split('\n')) {
   644	    24	        let obj;
   645	    25	        try {
   646	    26	          obj = JSON.parse(line);
   647	    27	        } catch {
   648	    28	          continue;
   649	    29	        }
   650	    30	        const val = String(matcher.path)
   651	    31	          .split('.')
   652	    32	          .reduce((o, k) => (o == null ? undefined : o[k]), obj);
   653	    33	        if (val === matcher.equals) return true;
   654	    34	      }
   655	    35	      return false;
   656	    36	    }
   657	    37	    default:
   658	    38	      return false;
   659	    39	  }
   660	    40	}
   661	    41	
   662	    42	/**
   663	    43	 * Classify harness output. Structured evidence first (text and exit code are
   664	    44	 * ignored when a recognized structured signal is present); text signatures as
   665	    45	 * the fallback tier; exit code only breaks the no-match tie — a generic nonzero
   666	    46	 * exit is never usage-limit on its own.
   667	    47	 * @param {{text?: string, exitCode?: number, platform: string, table: any, structured?: {kind: string, errorType: string} | null}} input
   668	    48	 * @returns {{class: string, signatureId: string | null, confidence: string | null, resetHint: string | null, tried?: string[]}}
   669	    49	 */
   670	    50	export function classify({ text = '', exitCode = 0, platform, table, structured = null }) {
   671	    51	  if (structured) {
   672	    52	    if (structured.kind === 'stop-failure') {
   673	    53	      const cls = STOP_FAILURE_MAP[structured.errorType] ?? 'other-error';
   674	    54	      return { class: cls, signatureId: `structured:${structured.errorType}`, confidence: 'high', resetHint: null };
   675	    55	    }
   676	    56	    if (structured.kind === 'exec-json') {
   677	    57	      const cls = structured.errorType === 'usage_limit' ? 'usage-limit' : 'other-error';
   678	    58	      return { class: cls, signatureId: `structured:${structured.errorType}`, confidence: 'high', resetHint: null };
   679	    59	    }
   680	    60	    // Unrecognized structured kind: fall through to the text tier.
   681	    61	  }
   682	    62	
   683	    63	  const stripped = text.replace(ANSI, '');
   684	    64	  const candidates = table.signatures.filter((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.platform === platform);
   685	    65	  /** @type {any} */
   686	    66	  let best = null;
   687	    67	  for (const s of candidates) {
   688	    68	    if (!matches(s.matcher, stripped)) continue;
   689	    69	    if (best === null || PRECEDENCE.indexOf(s.class) < PRECEDENCE.indexOf(best.class)) best = s;
   690	    70	  }
   691	    71	
   692	    72	  if (best) {
   693	    73	    let resetHint = null;
   694	    74	    if (typeof best.resetHint === 'string') {
   695	    75	      const m = stripped.match(new RegExp(best.resetHint));
   696	    76	      if (m) resetHint = m[1] ?? m[0];
   697	    77	    }
   698	    78	    return { class: best.class, signatureId: best.id, confidence: best.confidence, resetHint };
   699	    79	  }
   700	    80	
   701	    81	  return {
   702	    82	    class: exitCode !== 0 ? 'other-error' : 'ok',
   703	    83	    signatureId: null,
   704	    84	    confidence: null,
   705	    85	    resetHint: null,
   706	    86	  };
   707	    87	}

   784	     6	// RED — resolver entry-level avoidance (subtask l1-resolver-entries, part A).

   786	     8	// Source of truth: docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md
   787	     9	//   §"Model-level failover (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 4)" and
   788	    10	//   §"Acceptance constraints" constraint 2; docs/design/core.md §Module APIs
   789	    11	//   (resolveRoles). Mirrors the idioms in tests/unit/resolve.test.mjs.
   790	    12	//
   791	    13	// CONTRACT (pinned here):
   792	    14	//   A1. NEW OPT. resolveRoles opts gain `avoidEntries: [{platform, model}]`,
   793	    15	//       alongside the existing platform-level `avoid[]`. A chain entry is
   794	    16	//       skipped by this rule ONLY when BOTH platform AND model match an
   795	    17	//       avoidEntries item — a different model on the same platform stays
   796	    18	//       selectable (that is the whole point: model-level, not platform-level).
   797	    19	//   A2. AUDIT. An entry-avoided skip is recorded in skipped[] with a DISTINCT
   798	    20	//       why: 'avoided-entry' (mirrors the existing skip-recording idiom:
   799	    21	//       {chainIndex, platform, model, why}). The platform-level rule keeps its
   800	    22	//       own why 'avoided' — the two are never conflated.
   801	    23	//   A3. NAMED TEST (constraint 2). A chain whose first entry is codex/<A> and
   802	    24	//       second codex/<B>, with avoidEntries [{platform:'codex', model:'<A>'}],
   803	    25	//       selects <B> on the SAME platform (mode stays native), with no
   804	    26	//       platform-wide avoidance.
   805	    27	//   A4. COMPOSITION. avoidEntries composes with avoid[]: an entry on an AVOIDED
   806	    28	//       PLATFORM is skipped by the platform rule (why 'avoided'); entry
   807	    29	//       avoidance never resurrects a platform-avoided entry.
   808	    30	//   A5. EFFORT SUFFIX. Config chain strings are "platform/model[@effort]"
   809	    31	//       (matrix.mjs parses model + effort apart). avoidEntries matches on MODEL
   810	    32	//       IDENTITY regardless of any @effort suffix — model is what's
   811	    33	//       unavailable, not a specific effort. So an avoidEntries model carrying an
   812	    34	//       @effort suffix matches a parsed entry of the same base model, and effort
   813	    35	//       differences never prevent (or force) a match.
   814	    36	//   A6. EXHAUSTION. All entries avoided → the existing 'unavailable' path fires
   815	    37	//       (platform/model null, full skipped[]), never a crash.
   816	    38	//   A7. BACKWARD COMPAT. Omitted OR empty avoidEntries → behavior identical to
   817	    39	//       today (regression negatives, GREEN both today and after).
   818	    40	//
   819	    41	// PROOF-OF-RED: avoidEntries does not exist yet, so resolveRoles ignores it and
   820	    42	// selects the FIRST platform-eligible entry. The RED tests assert the
   821	    43	// entry-avoided selection / skip audit, which fails today (the avoided entry is
   822	    44	// still chosen). Tests labeled GREEN are invariants that hold both today and
   823	    45	// after and guard against over-broad matching.
   824	    46	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   825	    47	
   826	    48	const e = (/** @type {string} */ platform, /** @type {string} */ model, /** @type {string | null} */ effort = null) => ({ platform, model, effort });
   827	    49	const PLATFORMS = { 'claude-code': {}, codex: {}, cursor: {} };
   828	    50	const DEFAULTS = { 'claude-code': 'claude-fable-5', codex: 'gpt-5.6-sol', cursor: 'composer' };
   829	    51	const oneRole = (/** @type {any[]} */ chain) => ({ schema: 'baton/config@1', roles: { r: chain }, platforms: { ...PLATFORMS }, defaults: { ...DEFAULTS } });
   830	    52	
   831	    53	// ===========================================================================
   832	    54	describe('resolveRoles — entry-level avoidance: the named same-platform-next-entry test (A3)', () => {
   833	    55	  it('RED: codex/<A> avoided → codex/<B> selected on the SAME platform, mode native, no platform-wide avoidance', () => {
   834	    56	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   835	    57	      config: oneRole([e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh')]),
   836	    58	      to: 'codex',
   837	    59	      avoidEntries: [{ platform: 'codex', model: 'gpt-5.5' }],
   838	    60	    });
   839	    61	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, 'codex', 'the SAME platform stays selectable');
   840	    62	    assert.equal(assignments.r.model, 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'the next MODEL on that platform is chosen');
   841	    63	    assert.equal(assignments.r.mode, 'native', 'mode stays native (no platform failover)');
   842	    64	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, 1);
   843	    65	  });
   844	    66	
   845	    67	  it('RED: the passed-over entry is audited in skipped[] with why "avoided-entry"', () => {
   846	    68	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   847	    69	      config: oneRole([e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh')]),
   848	    70	      to: 'codex',
   849	    71	      avoidEntries: [{ platform: 'codex', model: 'gpt-5.5' }],
   850	    72	    });
   851	    73	    const skip = assignments.r.skipped.find((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.why === 'avoided-entry');
   852	    74	    assert.ok(skip, 'a distinct why "avoided-entry" records the entry-level skip');
   853	    75	    assert.equal(skip.platform, 'codex');
   854	    76	    assert.equal(skip.model, 'gpt-5.5');
   855	    77	    assert.equal(skip.chainIndex, 0);
   856	    78	  });
   857	    79	});
   858	    80	
   859	    81	// ===========================================================================
   860	    82	describe('resolveRoles — entry avoidance is MODEL-specific, not platform-wide (A1)', () => {
   861	    83	  it('GREEN guard: avoidEntries for a DIFFERENT model on the platform does NOT skip the head entry', () => {
   862	    84	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   863	    85	      config: oneRole([e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh')]),
   864	    86	      to: 'codex',
   865	    87	      avoidEntries: [{ platform: 'codex', model: 'gpt-5.6-sol' }],
   866	    88	    });
   867	    89	    assert.equal(assignments.r.model, 'gpt-5.5', 'a non-matching model avoidance must not skip a different model');
   868	    90	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, 0);
   869	    91	    assert.ok(!assignments.r.skipped.some((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.why === 'avoided-entry'), 'no entry was avoided');
   870	    92	  });
   871	    93	});
   872	    94	
   873	    95	// ===========================================================================
   874	    96	describe('resolveRoles — composition with platform-level avoid[] (A4)', () => {
   875	    97	  it('RED: platform-avoided entry keeps why "avoided"; a same-platform entry-avoided one is skipped "avoided-entry"; the survivor is chosen', () => {
   876	    98	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   877	    99	      config: oneRole([
   878	   100	        e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh'), // platform-avoided
   879	   101	        e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5'), // entry-avoided
   880	   102	        e('claude-code', 'claude-opus-4-8'), // survivor
   881	   103	      ]),
   882	   104	      to: 'claude-code',

   884	   106	      avoidEntries: [{ platform: 'claude-code', model: 'claude-fable-5' }],
   885	   107	    });
   886	   108	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, 'claude-code');
   887	   109	    assert.equal(assignments.r.model, 'claude-opus-4-8', 'the entry-avoided model is NOT resurrected; the survivor is chosen');
   888	   110	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, 2);
   889	   111	
   890	   112	    const codexSkip = assignments.r.skipped.find((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.platform === 'codex');
   891	   113	    assert.equal(codexSkip.why, 'avoided', 'a platform-avoided entry keeps the platform rule why "avoided"');
   892	   114	    const entrySkip = assignments.r.skipped.find((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.platform === 'claude-code' && s.model === 'claude-fable-5');
   893	   115	    assert.equal(entrySkip.why, 'avoided-entry', 'the same-platform model-avoided entry uses why "avoided-entry"');
   894	   116	  });
   895	   117	});
   896	   118	
   897	   119	// ===========================================================================
   898	   120	describe('resolveRoles — avoidEntries model matches regardless of @effort suffix (A5)', () => {

   905	   127	    });

   907	   129	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, 1);
   908	   130	  });

  1057	     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
  1058	     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';

  1064	     8	
  1065	     9	// The REAL shipped table (fold C1-2): the negative/near-miss corpus is run
  1066	    10	// through core/data/signatures.v1.json via the same loader the built-in pin
  1067	    11	// tests use, so an overbroad shipped regex that false-positives "usage limit"
  1068	    12	// prose is caught here — not just against an inline BASE table.

  1073	    17	
  1074	    18	// ===========================================================================
  1075	    19	// Contract choices for detect/classifier.mjs. Source of truth:
  1076	    20	// docs/design/core.md §Module APIs (classify signature + return shape),
  1077	    21	// §"signatures/classifier" test list; plan §Core engine — Limit detector
  1078	    22	// (structured-first, exit-code rule iteration 3, ANSI strip, precedence,
  1079	    23	// reset-hint extraction, negative corpus). classify is PURE.
  1080	    24	//
  1081	    25	// SIGNATURE (input): classify({text, exitCode, platform, table, structured}).
  1082	    26	//   - `table` is the loadSignatures return value; classify reads table.signatures
  1083	    27	//     (a FLAT array), and only applies signatures whose `platform` === platform.
  1084	    28	//   - Tables here are built inline (plain data) so this file's red phase is
  1085	    29	//     attributable to classifier.mjs alone (not signatures.mjs / the data file).
  1086	    30	//
  1087	    31	// RETURN: { class, signatureId, confidence, resetHint }  (tried? is added by the
  1088	    32	//   detect command under --explain, not by classify itself, so it is NOT pinned
  1089	    33	//   here). class ∈ 'ok'|'usage-limit'|'auth'|'throttle'|'other-error'.
  1090	    34	//
  1091	    35	// PINS:
  1092	    36	//   1. STRUCTURED-FIRST. TWO structured kinds are recognized, and for BOTH the
  1093	    37	//      TEXT AND EXIT CODE ARE IGNORED (structured evidence wins outright):
  1094	    38	//        (a) {kind:'stop-failure', errorType} — Claude Code StopFailure enum
  1095	    39	//            (platform-notes.md snapshot). errorType maps (fold C1-4 completes
  1096	    40	//            the enum — invalid_request / model_not_found / max_output_tokens
  1097	    41	//            were previously omitted):
  1098	    42	//               rate_limit                         -> usage-limit
  1099	    43	//               overloaded                         -> throttle
  1100	    44	//               authentication_failed              -> auth
  1101	    45	//               oauth_org_not_allowed              -> auth
  1102	    46	//               billing_error                      -> auth
  1103	    47	//               invalid_request                    -> other-error
  1104	    48	//               model_not_found                    -> other-error
  1105	    49	//               server_error                       -> other-error
  1106	    50	//               max_output_tokens                  -> other-error
  1107	    51	//               unknown / any unrecognized value   -> other-error
  1108	    52	//        (b) {kind:'exec-json', errorType} — Codex `codex exec --json` limit
  1109	    53	//            evidence (fold C1-4). errorType 'usage_limit' -> usage-limit.
  1110	    54	//      signatureId = `structured:<errorType>` for both kinds (rate_limit pinned
  1111	    55	//      exactly to 'structured:rate_limit'; codex usage_limit -> 'structured:usage_limit').
  1112	    56	//      Structured confidence = 'high'. resetHint = null.
  1113	    57	//      A structured value with a kind OTHER than these two (e.g. 'some-other-event')
  1114	    58	//      is ignored and classification falls through to the text/exit-code tiers.
  1115	    59	//   2. TEXT TIER. ANSI escape sequences are stripped before matching.
  1116	    60	//      Precedence when multiple signatures match: usage-limit > auth > throttle
  1117	    61	//      > other-error. A matched signature's `confidence` and `id` are carried
  1118	    62	//      through (signatureId === sig.id, confidence === sig.confidence).
  1119	    63	//   3. RESET-HINT EXTRACTION. When the matched signature carries a `resetHint`
  1120	    64	//      regex, classify runs it against the (ANSI-stripped) text and returns
  1121	    65	//      match[1] ?? match[0] (first capture group if present, else whole match);
  1122	    66	//      no regex or no match -> resetHint null. A matched signature with no
  1123	    67	//      resetHint -> resetHint null.
  1124	    68	//   4. EXIT-CODE RULE. A matching signature WINS regardless of exit code. With
  1125	    69	//      NO matching signature: nonzero exit -> other-error (NEVER usage-limit);
  1126	    70	//      zero exit -> ok. No-match verdicts carry signatureId null, resetHint null.
  1127	    71	//   5. json-field matcher parses each LINE of text as JSON and compares a dotted
  1128	    72	//      path to `equals`.
  1129	    73	// ===========================================================================
  1130	    74	
  1131	    75	const ANSI_RED = '\x1b[31m';
  1132	    76	const ANSI_RESET = '\x1b[0m';
  1133	    77

  1174	    16	//
  1175	    17	//   A. TABLE SHAPE (loadSignatures return value): a flat object
  1176	    18	//        { schema: 'baton/signatures@1', updated: <ISO-ish date string>,
  1177	    19	//          signatures: Signature[] }
  1178	    20	//      Signatures are a FLAT array (not grouped by platform); each carries its
  1179	    21	//      own `platform`. The classifier filters by that field. Override-by-id and
  1180	    22	//      the classifier both operate over `table.signatures`.
  1181	    23	//
  1182	    24	//   B. SIGNATURE SHAPE:
  1183	    25	//        { id: string,            // unique; overlay merge key
  1184	    26	//          platform: 'claude-code'|'codex'|'cursor',
  1185	    27	//          class: 'usage-limit'|'auth'|'throttle'|'other-error',
  1186	    28	//          confidence: 'high'|'medium'|'low',
  1187	    29	//          matcher: Matcher,
  1188	    30	//          resetHint?: string }   // OPTIONAL regex (string) used by the
  1189	    31	//                                 // classifier to extract a reset hint.
  1190	    32	//      Matcher kinds (the only three accepted):
  1191	    33	//        { kind:'substring', value:string }
  1192	    34	//        { kind:'regex', pattern:string, flags?:string }
  1193	    35	//        { kind:'json-field', path:string /* dotted */, equals:<value> }
  1194	    36	//
  1195	    37	//   C. VALIDATION FAILURES THROW. loadSignatures returns a Table on success;
  1196	    38	//      an invalid built-in or overlay table (bad matcher kind, over-long
  1197	    39	//      regex, syntactically-invalid regex, backreference, pathological nested
  1198	    40	//      quantifier, unknown platform key) throws an Error. Error messages are
  1199	    41	//      asserted only with permissive keyword regexes.
  1200	    42	//      - regex pattern length cap: 200 chars (201 rejected).
  1201	    43	//      - a syntactically INVALID regex is rejected with a PATHED error — the
  1202	    44	//        message names the offending signature id (fold C1-3).
  1203	    45	//      - backreferences (\1) rejected (PIN: the open lookbehind question is
  1204	    46	//        resolved to "backreferences rejected"; lookbehind is left UNPINNED so
  1205	    47	//        the implementer keeps latitude there).
  1206	    48	//      - a pathological nested-quantifier construct like (a+)+ is rejected as a
  1207	    49	//        non-linear-time / catastrophic-backtracking risk (fold C1-3). classify()
  1208	    50	//        stays SYNCHRONOUS (no options.timeBudgetMs); the runtime time-guard is
  1209	    51	//        achieved BY CONSTRUCTION — because the lint forbids such patterns from
  1210	    52	//        ever shipping or being overlaid, classify() cannot hang on adversarial
  1211	    53	//        input. The same lints apply to the OVERLAY path.
  1212	    54	//      - unknown platform key (not one of the three) rejected.
  1213	    55	//
  1214	    56	//   D. OVERLAY (overlayPath): merged over the built-in by signature `id`,
  1215	    57	//      LATER WINS — an overlay id equal to a built-in id replaces it; a new
  1216	    58	//      overlay id is appended. A missing/undefined overlayPath = no overlay.

  1218	    60	//   E. BUILT-IN PIN: the real shipped file core/data/signatures.v1.json is

  1231	    73	// Apply a matcher the same way the classifier is contracted to, so the built-in
  1232	    74	// content pins are robust to a substring-vs-regex authoring choice for the

  1234	    76	function matcherMatches(matcher, text) {
  1235	    77	  if (!matcher) return false;
  1236	    78	  if (matcher.kind === 'substring') return text.includes(matcher.value);
  1237	    79	  if (matcher.kind === 'regex') return new RegExp(matcher.pattern, matcher.flags || '').test(text);
  1238	    80	  if (matcher.kind === 'json-field') {
  1239	    81	    for (const line of text.split('\n')) {
  1240	    82	      let obj;
  1241	    83	      try {
  1242	    84	        obj = JSON.parse(line);
  1243	    85	      } catch {
  1244	    86	        continue;
  1245	    87	      }
  1246	    88	      const val = String(matcher.path).split('.').reduce((o, k) => (o == null ? undefined : o[k]), obj);
  1247	    89	      if (val === matcher.equals) return true;
  1248	    90	    }
  1249	    91	    return false;
  1250	    92	  }
  1251	    93	  return false;
  1252	    94	}

  1290	     8	/**
  1291	     9	 * Is a quantified group's body ambiguous? Alternation, another quantifier, or
  1292	    10	 * any nested group inside a `(…)+`/`(…)*`/`(…){` makes backtracking
  1293	    11	 * super-linear in the worst case — conservatively rejected (gate-2 fix 6:
  1294	    12	 * the old lint accepted `(a|aa)+$` and the `(a|a)*x` live repro).
  1295	    13	 * @param {string} body
  1296	    14	 */
  1297	    15	function riskyGroupBody(body) {
  1298	    16	  // Strip a non-capture/lookaround/named marker so `(?:abc)+` reads as `abc`.
  1299	    17	  const b = body.replace(/^\?(?:<=|<!|:|=|!|<[^>]*>)/, '');
  1300	    18	  let inClass = false;
  1301	    19	  for (let i = 0; i < b.length; i++) {
  1302	    20	    const c = b[i];
  1303	    21	    if (c === '\\') {
  1304	    22	      i++;
  1305	    23	      continue;
  1306	    24	    }
  1307	    25	    if (inClass) {
  1308	    26	      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
  1309	    27	      continue;
  1310	    28	    }
  1311	    29	    if (c === '[') {
  1312	    30	      inClass = true;
  1313	    31	      continue;
  1314	    32	    }
  1315	    33	    if (c === '|' || c === '+' || c === '*' || c === '{' || c === '?' || c === '(' || c === ')') return true;
  1316	    34	  }
  1317	    35	  return false;
  1318	    36	}
  1319	    37	
  1320	    38	/**
  1321	    39	 * Structural scan: walk the pattern (class- and escape-aware), and for every
  1322	    40	 * group followed by an unbounded quantifier check its full body — any nesting
  1323	    41	 * depth — for ambiguity. Returns a problem string or null.
  1324	    42	 * @param {string} pattern
  1325	    43	 */
  1326	    44	function scanQuantifiedGroups(pattern) {
  1327	    45	  /** @type {number[]} */
  1328	    46	  const opens = [];
  1329	    47	  let inClass = false;
  1330	    48	  for (let i = 0; i < pattern.length; i++) {
  1331	    49	    const c = pattern[i];
  1332	    50	    if (c === '\\') {
  1333	    51	      i++;
  1334	    52	      continue;
  1335	    53	    }
  1336	    54	    if (inClass) {
  1337	    55	      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
  1338	    56	      continue;
  1339	    57	    }
  1340	    58	    if (c === '[') {
  1341	    59	      inClass = true;
  1342	    60	      continue;
  1343	    61	    }
  1344	    62	    if (c === '(') {
  1345	    63	      opens.push(i);
  1346	    64	      continue;
  1347	    65	    }
  1348	    66	    if (c === ')') {
  1349	    67	      const start = opens.pop();
  1350	    68	      if (start === undefined) continue; // unbalanced — new RegExp() reports it
  1351	    69	      const next = pattern[i + 1];
  1352	    70	      if (next === '+' || next === '*' || next === '{') {
  1353	    71	        const body = pattern.slice(start + 1, i);
  1354	    72	        if (riskyGroupBody(body)) {
  1355	    73	          return `quantified group "(${body})" contains alternation, another quantifier, or a nested group`;
  1356	    74	        }
  1357	    75	      }
  1358	    76	    }
  1359	    77	  }
  1360	    78	  return null;
  1361	    79	}
  1362	    80	
  1363	    81	/**
  1364	    82	 * Approximate character-set overlap between two simple-atom alphabets.
  1365	    83	 * Descriptors: {all:true} (dot / broad negated shorthand) | {sh:'d'|'w'|'s'} |
  1366	    84	 * {lit:Set<string>}. Returns true unless the two sets are PROVABLY disjoint —
  1367	    85	 * conservative for untrusted overlays (iter-4 I4).
  1368	    86	 * @param {any} a @param {any} b
  1369	    87	 */
  1370	    88	function alphasOverlap(a, b) {
  1371	    89	  if (!a || !b) return false;
  1372	    90	  if (a.all || b.all) return true;
  1373	    91	  if (a.sh && b.sh) {
  1374	    92	    if (a.sh === b.sh) return true;
  1375	    93	    // \s is disjoint from \d and \w; \d ⊂ \w so they overlap.
  1376	    94	    return !(a.sh === 's' || b.sh === 's');
  1377	    95	  }
  1378	    96	  if (a.lit && b.lit) {
  1379	    97	    for (const c of a.lit) if (b.lit.has(c)) return true;
  1380	    98	    return false;
  1381	    99	  }
  1382	   100	  const sh = a.sh ? a.sh : b.sh;
  1383	   101	  const lit = a.lit ? a.lit : b.lit;
  1384	   102	  for (const c of lit) {
  1385	   103	    if (sh === 'd' && c >= '0' && c <= '9') return true;
  1386	   104	    if (sh === 'w' && /[A-Za-z0-9_]/.test(c)) return true;
  1387	   105	    if (sh === 's' && /\s/.test(c)) return true;
  1388	   106	  }
  1389	   107	  return false;
  1390	   108	}
  1391	   109	
  1392	   110	/**
  1393	   111	 * Structural scan for a run of ≥2 CONSECUTIVE atoms, each with an UNBOUNDED
  1394	   112	 * quantifier (`*`, `+`, `{n,}`), whose alphabets overlap — `.*.*…`, `\d*\d*…`,
  1395	   113	 * `\(*\(*…`, `\n*\n*…`, `[a-z]*[a-z]*…`. Such a chain partitions the same input
  1396	   114	 * combinatorially and backtracks super-linearly, yet is invisible to the
  1397	   115	 * quantified-GROUP scan; and because a finite probe alphabet cannot guarantee a
  1398	   116	 * non-matching tail for every atom (`.` accepts almost everything), rejecting
  1399	   117	 * the STRUCTURE at load is the reliable guard (iter-4 I4, reviewer-a #7 /
  1400	   118	 * reviewer-b #3). Groups reset the run (the group scan covers quantified groups;
  1401	   119	 * a group's overlap with a simple atom is not decided here). Returns a problem
  1402	   120	 * string or null.
  1403	   121	 * @param {string} pattern
  1404	   122	 * @returns {string | null}
  1405	   123	 */
  1406	   124	function scanChainedQuantifiedAtoms(pattern) {
  1407	   125	  const p = pattern;
  1408	   126	  /** @type {any} */
  1409	   127	  let prev = null; // alpha of the previous atom IF it carried an unbounded quantifier, else null
  1410	   128	
  1411	   129	  for (let i = 0; i < p.length; ) {
  1412	   130	    const c = p[i];
  1413	   131	    // Chain-breakers: alternation and anchors end any run.
  1414	   132	    if (c === '|' || c === '^' || c === '$') {
  1415	   133	      prev = null;
  1416	   134	      i += 1;
  1417	   135	      continue;
  1418	   136	    }
  1419	   137	    /** @type {any} */
  1420	   138	    let alpha = null;
  1421	   139	    let isGroup = false;
  1422	   140	    if (c === '\\') {
  1423	   141	      const n = p[i + 1];
  1424	   142	      // decode length of the escape token
  1425	   143	      let len = 2;
  1426	   144	      if (n === 'x') len = 4;
  1427	   145	      else if (n === 'u') len = p[i + 2] === '{' ? (p.indexOf('}', i) - i + 1) : 6;
  1428	   146	      if (n === 'd' || n === 'w' || n === 's') alpha = { sh: n };
  1429	   147	      else if (n === 'D' || n === 'W' || n === 'S' || n === 'b' || n === 'B') alpha = { all: true }; // broad/negated or anchor-ish → conservative
  1430	   148	      else alpha = { lit: new Set([decodeEscape(p.slice(i, i + len))]) }; // \(, \n, \t, \xNN, \uNNNN → the literal char
  1431	   149	      i += Math.max(2, len);
  1432	   150	    } else if (c === '[') {
  1433	   151	      const end = classEnd(p, i);
  1434	   152	      const body = p.slice(i + 1, end);
  1435	   153	      alpha = body.startsWith('^') ? { all: true } : classAlpha(body);
  1436	   154	      i = end + 1;
  1437	   155	    } else if (c === '(') {
  1438	   156	      // Skip the whole group (balance-aware); recurse into its body for chains.
  1439	   157	      const end = groupEnd(p, i);
  1440	   158	      const inner = scanChainedQuantifiedAtoms(p.slice(i + 1, end));
  1441	   159	      if (inner) return inner;
  1442	   160	      isGroup = true;
  1443	   161	      i = end + 1;
  1444	   162	    } else if (c === '.') {
  1445	   163	      alpha = { all: true };
  1446	   164	      i += 1;
  1447	   165	    } else {
  1448	   166	      alpha = { lit: new Set([c]) };
  1449	   167	      i += 1;
  1450	   168	    }
  1451	   169	
  1452	   170	    // Read an optional quantifier and classify it as unbounded or not.
  1453	   171	    let unbounded = false;
  1454	   172	    const q = p[i];
  1455	   173	    if (q === '*' || q === '+') {
  1456	   174	      unbounded = true;
  1457	   175	      i += 1;
  1458	   176	    } else if (q === '{') {
  1459	   177	      const close = p.indexOf('}', i);
  1460	   178	      if (close !== -1) {
  1461	   179	        const spec = p.slice(i + 1, close);
  1462	   180	        // {n,} (no upper bound) is unbounded; {n} and {n,m} are bounded.
  1463	   181	        unbounded = /^\d+,\s*$/.test(spec);
  1464	   182	        i = close + 1;
  1465	   183	      }
  1466	   184	    } else if (q === '?') {
  1467	   185	      i += 1;
  1468	   186	    }
  1469	   187	    // consume a lazy/possessive modifier if present
  1470	   188	    if (p[i] === '?' || p[i] === '+') i += 1;
  1471	   189	
  1472	   190	    if (isGroup) {
  1473	   191	      // A group breaks the simple-atom run.
  1474	   192	      prev = null;
  1475	   193	      continue;
  1476	   194	    }
  1477	   195	    // prev + current = a run of ≥2 CONSECUTIVE overlapping unbounded-quantified
  1478	   196	    // atoms → super-linear backtracking. Reject.
  1479	   197	    if (unbounded && prev && alphasOverlap(prev, alpha)) {
  1480	   198	      return 'chained overlapping unbounded quantifiers (e.g. `.*.*`, `\\d*\\d*`, `\\(*\\(*`) backtrack super-linearly';
  1481	   199	    }
  1482	   200	    prev = unbounded ? alpha : null;
  1483	   201	  }
  1484	   202	  return null;
  1485	   203	}
  1486	   204	
  1487	   205	/** Decode a single-char escape token (`\(`, `\n`, `\t`, `\xNN`, `\uNNNN`, `\u{...}`) to its literal char. @param {string} tok */
  1488	   206	function decodeEscape(tok) {
  1489	   207	  const rest = tok.slice(1);
  1490	   208	  if (rest[0] === 'x') return String.fromCharCode(parseInt(rest.slice(1, 3), 16));
  1491	   209	  if (rest[0] === 'u') {
  1492	   210	    const h = rest[1] === '{' ? rest.slice(2, rest.indexOf('}')) : rest.slice(1, 5);
  1493	   211	    try {
  1494	   212	      return String.fromCodePoint(parseInt(h, 16));
  1495	   213	    } catch {
  1496	   214	      return rest[0];
  1497	   215	    }
  1498	   216	  }
  1499	   217	  const named = { n: '\n', t: '\t', r: '\r', f: '\f', v: '\v', 0: '\0' };
  1500	   218	  return named[/** @type {'n'} */ (rest[0])] ?? rest[0];
  1501	   219	}
  1502	   220	
  1503	   221	/** Index of the `]` closing a class opened at `open` (a `]` immediately after `[`/`[^` is a literal). @param {string} p @param {number} open */
  1504	   222	function classEnd(p, open) {
  1505	   223	  let i = open + 1;
  1506	   224	  if (p[i] === '^') i += 1;
  1507	   225	  if (p[i] === ']') i += 1; // leading ] is literal
  1508	   226	  for (; i < p.length; i++) {
  1509	   227	    if (p[i] === '\\') {
  1510	   228	      i += 1;
  1511	   229	      continue;
  1512	   230	    }
  1513	   231	    if (p[i] === ']') return i;
  1514	   232	  }
  1515	   233	  return p.length - 1;
  1516	   234	}
  1517	   235	
  1518	   236	/** Index of the `)` closing a group opened at `open` (balance-aware, class/escape-safe). @param {string} p @param {number} open */
  1519	   237	function groupEnd(p, open) {
  1520	   238	  let depth = 0;
  1521	   239	  let inClass = false;
  1522	   240	  for (let i = open; i < p.length; i++) {
  1523	   241	    const c = p[i];
  1524	   242	    if (c === '\\') {
  1525	   243	      i += 1;
  1526	   244	      continue;
  1527	   245	    }
  1528	   246	    if (inClass) {
  1529	   247	      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
  1530	   248	      continue;
  1531	   249	    }
  1532	   250	    if (c === '[') inClass = true;
  1533	   251	    else if (c === '(') depth += 1;
  1534	   252	    else if (c === ')') {
  1535	   253	      depth -= 1;
  1536	   254	      if (depth === 0) return i;
  1537	   255	    }
  1538	   256	  }
  1539	   257	  return p.length - 1;
  1540	   258	}
  1541	   259	
  1542	   260	/** Approximate alphabet of a (non-negated) class body: literals + range endpoints + shorthands. @param {string} body */

  2897	  7285	3. [blocking] — The failover chain is underspecified relative to the receive transaction. The plan says `detect → finalize → remap → receive → relaunch` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:66)), but `baton remap` is read-only ([core/src/commands/remap.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/remap.mjs:6)), and `receive` requires prepare/commit with identical bound inputs and session/root/probe/git state ([core/src/commands/receive.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/receive.mjs:33), [core/src/commands/receive.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/receive.mjs:64)). Probe cache and git state are token inputs ([core/src/receive/txn.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/receive/txn.mjs:65)), so any supervisor write, probe refresh, ff-sync, or smoke artifact change between prepare and commit can invalidate the token. — Fix: Replace the chain with an exact transaction: freeze child output, classify, finalize or degraded-open receive, refresh probes before prepare, prepare+commit with identical flags/session/root and no intervening writes, then relaunch.   2899	  7287	4. [blocking] — “Role’s next chain entry” is not supported for model-level failures, including the degradation case in this review. If `codex/gpt-5.6-sol` is account-tier rejected but Codex itself is usable, the loop can retry the same rejected model forever or avoid all Codex entries and skip valid `codex/gpt-5.5` fallbacks. — Fix: Add model-entry failure state or an `avoidEntries[]` mechanism, classify model-unavailable separately from usage-limit, and test `gpt-5.6-sol rejected → codex/gpt-5.5 selected` without platform-wide avoidance.

  2901	  7289	5. [major] — The worktree discipline lacks corruption guards. The plan says two persistent worktrees are gitignored and branch-jailed, with only the merger writing main ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:78)), but current `.gitignore` does not ignore `.worktrees/` ([.gitignore](/Users/saksham/baton/.gitignore:1)).   3010	    11	//
  3011	    12	// INPUT: resolveRoles({config, to, avoid = [], nativeOnly = false, probes = null}).
  3012	    13	//   `config` is the PARSED config (roles as arrays of {platform, model, effort},
  3013	    14	//   as loadConfig returns) — built inline here so this file's red phase is
  3014	    15	//   attributable to resolve.mjs alone (not matrix.mjs).
  3015	    16	//
  3016	    17	// RETURN: { assignments: {[role]: Assignment}, notes: [] }.
  3017	    18	//   Assignment = {platform, model, effort, mode, chainIndex, skipped[], degraded?}.
  3018	    19	//   modes: 'native' | 'delegated' | 'forced-default' | 'unavailable'.
  3019	    20	//
  3020	    21	// PINS:
  3021	    22	//   1. CHAIN WALK. For each role, walk entries in order; the FIRST eligible
  3022	    23	//      (surviving) entry is selected. skipped[] records each passed-over entry
  3023	    24	//      as {chainIndex, platform, model, why} (extra fields tolerated).
  3024	    25	//      why values: 'avoided' | 'unknown-platform' | 'disabled' | 'rate-limited' | 'unauthenticated'.
  3025	    26	//   2. ELIGIBILITY / skip reasons:
  3026	    27	//        - platform ∈ avoid                          -> skip, why 'avoided'
  3027	    28	//        - platform NOT a key of config.platforms    -> skip, why 'unknown-platform'
  3028	    29	//        - config.platforms[p].enabled === false     -> skip, why 'disabled'
  3029	    30	//        - probes given & probes[p].outcome==='rate-limited' -> skip, why 'rate-limited'
  3030	    31	//        - probes given & capability==='installed' AND outcome!=='ok' (a VERIFIED
  3031	    32	//          auth/reachability FAILURE)                 -> skip, why 'unauthenticated'
  3032	    33	//          (iter-3 F1: a capped SUCCESS {installed, ok} leaves auth UNVERIFIED —
  3033	    34	//          it stays selectable and is flagged degraded, it is NOT skipped)
  3034	    35	//      A platform with capability ∈ {authenticated, reachable} AND outcome
  3035	    36	//      ≠ 'rate-limited' is eligible.
  3036	    37	//   3. DEGRADED. If probes is a non-null object but has NO entry for the chosen
  3037	    38	//      platform (unverifiable), the entry is selectable with degraded === true.
  3038	    39	//      probes === null means OFFLINE resolution: still selectable, but every
  3039	    40	//      selection is flagged degraded (gate-2 iter-2 M3; plan: offline always degraded).
  3040	    41	//      Non-degraded assignments have a falsy `degraded` (absent or false).
  3041	    42	//   4. MODE. First surviving entry: mode 'native' iff entry.platform === to,
  3042	    43	//      else 'delegated'. chainIndex = that entry's index in the ORIGINAL chain.
  3043	    44	//   5. nativeOnly restricts consideration to entries with platform === to; if
  3044	    45	//      none survive it MAY fall back to config.defaults[to] with mode
  3045	    46	//      'forced-default' (platform=to, model=defaults[to], effort=null,
  3046	    47	//      chainIndex=null) — but ONLY when the destination `to` is itself
  3047	    48	//      probe-eligible (PIN 2). When `to` is probe-INELIGIBLE (e.g. rate-limited),
  3048	    49	//      the forced-default is SUPPRESSED and the role resolves 'unavailable', the
  3049	    50	//      ineligibility audited in skipped[] (why 'rate-limited'). (fold C1-1)
  3050	    51	//   6. UNAVAILABLE. In normal (non-nativeOnly) mode, when NOTHING survives:
  3051	    52	//      mode 'unavailable', platform null, model null, effort null,
  3052	    53	//      chainIndex null, and skipped[] carries the full passed-over list.
  3053	    54	//   7. DETERMINISM. Same input -> deep-equal output; input config is not mutated.
  3054	    55	//      assignments object keys are in the CONFIG'S ROLE ORDER.
  3055	    56	//   8. avoid[] auto-population is the CALLER'S job; resolve only honors the list.
  3056	    57	// ===========================================================================
  3057	    58	
  3058	    59	const e = (platform, model, effort = null) => ({ platform, model, effort });
  3059	    60	
  3060	    61	const PLATFORMS = { 'claude-code': {}, codex: {}, cursor: {} };
  3061	    62	const DEFAULTS = { 'claude-code': 'claude-fable-5', codex: 'gpt-5.6-sol', cursor: 'composer' };
  3062	    63	
  3063	    64	// The full seven-role config in parsed form (mirrors the real baton.config.json).
  3064	    65	function fullConfig() {
  3065	    66	  return {
  3066	    67	    schema: 'baton/config@1',
  3067	    68	    roles: {
  3068	    69	      planner: [e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5')],
  3069	    70	      'plan-reviewer': [e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh'), e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5', 'xhigh')],
  3070	    71	      'test-author': [e('claude-code', 'claude-opus-4-8'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh')],
  3071	    72	      'test-verifier': [e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh'), e('claude-code', 'claude-opus-4-8')],
  3072	    73	      implementer: [e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh'), e('cursor', 'composer')],
  3073	    74	      'final-reviewer-a': [e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh')],
  3074	    75	      'final-reviewer-b': [e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5', 'xhigh'), e('claude-code', 'claude-opus-4-8')],
  3075	    76	    },
  3076	    77	    platforms: { ...PLATFORMS },
  3077	    78	    defaults: { ...DEFAULTS },
  3078	    79	  };
  3079	    80	}
  3080	    81	
  3081	    82	// A small single-role config helper.
  3082	    83	function oneRole(chain) {
  3083	    84	  return { schema: 'baton/config@1', roles: { r: chain }, platforms: { ...PLATFORMS }, defaults: { ...DEFAULTS } };
  3084	    85	}
  3085	    86	
  3086	    87	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { resolveRoles } from '../../core/src/roles/resolve.mjs';
     4	
     5	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     6	// RED — resolver entry-level avoidance (subtask l1-resolver-entries, part A).
     7	//
     8	// Source of truth: docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md
     9	//   §"Model-level failover (Gate-1 iteration 1, finding 4)" and
    10	//   §"Acceptance constraints" constraint 2; docs/design/core.md §Module APIs
    11	//   (resolveRoles). Mirrors the idioms in tests/unit/resolve.test.mjs.
    12	//
    13	// CONTRACT (pinned here):
    14	//   A1. NEW OPT. resolveRoles opts gain `avoidEntries: [{platform, model}]`,
    15	//       alongside the existing platform-level `avoid[]`. A chain entry is
    16	//       skipped by this rule ONLY when BOTH platform AND model match an
    17	//       avoidEntries item — a different model on the same platform stays
    18	//       selectable (that is the whole point: model-level, not platform-level).
    19	//   A2. AUDIT. An entry-avoided skip is recorded in skipped[] with a DISTINCT
    20	//       why: 'avoided-entry' (mirrors the existing skip-recording idiom:
    21	//       {chainIndex, platform, model, why}). The platform-level rule keeps its
    22	//       own why 'avoided' — the two are never conflated.
    23	//   A3. NAMED TEST (constraint 2). A chain whose first entry is codex/<A> and
    24	//       second codex/<B>, with avoidEntries [{platform:'codex', model:'<A>'}],
    25	//       selects <B> on the SAME platform (mode stays native), with no
    26	//       platform-wide avoidance.
    27	//   A4. COMPOSITION. avoidEntries composes with avoid[]: an entry on an AVOIDED
    28	//       PLATFORM is skipped by the platform rule (why 'avoided'); entry
    29	//       avoidance never resurrects a platform-avoided entry.
    30	//   A5. EFFORT SUFFIX. Config chain strings are "platform/model[@effort]"
    31	//       (matrix.mjs parses model + effort apart). avoidEntries matches on MODEL
    32	//       IDENTITY regardless of any @effort suffix — model is what's
    33	//       unavailable, not a specific effort. So an avoidEntries model carrying an
    34	//       @effort suffix matches a parsed entry of the same base model, and effort
    35	//       differences never prevent (or force) a match.
    36	//   A6. EXHAUSTION. All entries avoided → the existing 'unavailable' path fires
    37	//       (platform/model null, full skipped[]), never a crash.
    38	//   A7. BACKWARD COMPAT. Omitted OR empty avoidEntries → behavior identical to
    39	//       today (regression negatives, GREEN both today and after).
    40	//
    41	// PROOF-OF-RED: avoidEntries does not exist yet, so resolveRoles ignores it and
    42	// selects the FIRST platform-eligible entry. The RED tests assert the
    43	// entry-avoided selection / skip audit, which fails today (the avoided entry is
    44	// still chosen). Tests labeled GREEN are invariants that hold both today and
    45	// after and guard against over-broad matching.
    46	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    47

    49	const PLATFORMS = { 'claude-code': {}, codex: {}, cursor: {} };
    50	const DEFAULTS = { 'claude-code': 'claude-fable-5', codex: 'gpt-5.6-sol', cursor: 'composer' };
    51	const oneRole = (/** @type {any[]} */ chain) => ({ schema: 'baton/config@1', roles: { r: chain }, platforms: { ...PLATFORMS }, defaults: { ...DEFAULTS } });
    52

     7	import { loadSignatures } from '../../core/src/detect/signatures.mjs';
     8	import { classify } from '../../core/src/detect/classifier.mjs';
     9	
    10	// ===========================================================================
    11	// Contract choices for detect/signatures.mjs + core/data/signatures.v1.json.
    12	// Source of truth: docs/design/core.md §Module APIs (loadSignatures signature,
    13	// "validates, lints regexes"), §"signatures/classifier" test list; plan
    14	// §Core engine — Limit detector; docs/design/platform-notes.md (verified
    15	// limit strings). Readings this file PINS where the spec left room:
    16	//
    17	//   A. TABLE SHAPE (loadSignatures return value): a flat object
    18	//        { schema: 'baton/signatures@1', updated: <ISO-ish date string>,
    19	//          signatures: Signature[] }
    20	//      Signatures are a FLAT array (not grouped by platform); each carries its
    21	//      own `platform`. The classifier filters by that field. Override-by-id and
    22	//      the classifier both operate over `table.signatures`.
    23	//
    24	//   B. SIGNATURE SHAPE:
    25	//        { id: string,            // unique; overlay merge key
    26	//          platform: 'claude-code'|'codex'|'cursor',
    27	//          class: 'usage-limit'|'auth'|'throttle'|'other-error',
    28	//          confidence: 'high'|'medium'|'low',
    29	//          matcher: Matcher,
    30	//          resetHint?: string }   // OPTIONAL regex (string) used by the
    31	//                                 // classifier to extract a reset hint.
    32	//      Matcher kinds (the only three accepted):
    33	//        { kind:'substring', value:string }
    34	//        { kind:'regex', pattern:string, flags?:string }
    35	//        { kind:'json-field', path:string /* dotted */, equals:<value> }
    36	//
    37	//   C. VALIDATION FAILURES THROW. loadSignatures returns a Table on success;
    38	//      an invalid built-in or overlay table (bad matcher kind, over-long
    39	//      regex, syntactically-invalid regex, backreference, pathological nested
    40	//      quantifier, unknown platform key) throws an Error. Error messages are
    41	//      asserted only with permissive keyword regexes.
    42	//      - regex pattern length cap: 200 chars (201 rejected).
    43	//      - a syntactically INVALID regex is rejected with a PATHED error — the
    44	//        message names the offending signature id (fold C1-3).
    45	//      - backreferences (\1) rejected (PIN: the open lookbehind question is
    46	//        resolved to "backreferences rejected"; lookbehind is left UNPINNED so
    47	//        the implementer keeps latitude there).
    48	//      - a pathological nested-quantifier construct like (a+)+ is rejected as a
    49	//        non-linear-time / catastrophic-backtracking risk (fold C1-3). classify()
    50	//        stays SYNCHRONOUS (no options.timeBudgetMs); the runtime time-guard is
    51	//        achieved BY CONSTRUCTION — because the lint forbids such patterns from
    52	//        ever shipping or being overlaid, classify() cannot hang on adversarial
    53	//        input. The same lints apply to the OVERLAY path.
    54	//      - unknown platform key (not one of the three) rejected.
    55	//
    56	//   D. OVERLAY (overlayPath): merged over the built-in by signature `id`,
    57	//      LATER WINS — an overlay id equal to a built-in id replaces it; a new
    58	//      overlay id is appended. A missing/undefined overlayPath = no overlay.
    59	//
    60	//   E. BUILT-IN PIN: the real shipped file core/data/signatures.v1.json is
    61	//      loaded through the loader via node:fs (a minimal real-fs io = {fs}) so
    62	//      the shipped data — the verified per-platform strings from
    63	//      platform-notes.md — is pinned, not just the loader's mechanics.
    64	// ===========================================================================
    65	
    66	const here = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
    67	const repoRoot = join(here, '..', '..');
    68	const REAL_SIGNATURES_PATH = join(repoRoot, 'core', 'data', 'signatures.v1.json');
    69	
    70	// Minimal real-fs io: loadSignatures only needs fs.readFileSync / fs.existsSync.
    71	const realIo = { fs: nodeFs };
    72	
    73	// Apply a matcher the same way the classifier is contracted to, so the built-in
    74	// content pins are robust to a substring-vs-regex authoring choice for the
    75	// flexible cases (e.g. the 429 other-error entry).
    76	function matcherMatches(matcher, text) {
    77	  if (!matcher) return false;
    78	  if (matcher.kind === 'substring') return text.includes(matcher.value);
    79	  if (matcher.kind === 'regex') return new RegExp(matcher.pattern, matcher.flags || '').test(text);
    80	  if (matcher.kind === 'json-field') {
    81	    for (const line of text.split('\n')) {
    82	      let obj;
    83	      try {
    84	        obj = JSON.parse(line);
    85	      } catch {
    86	        continue;
    87	      }
    88	      const val = String(matcher.path).split('.').reduce((o, k) => (o == null ? undefined : o[k]), obj);
    89	      if (val === matcher.equals) return true;
    90	    }

     1	/**
     2	 * Pure, explainable role resolution over the committed matrix. Every decision
     3	 * is auditable: chosen entries carry their chainIndex; passed-over entries land
     4	 * in skipped[] with a why. avoid[] population is the caller's job.
     5	 * @param {{config: any, to: string, avoid?: string[], nativeOnly?: boolean, probes?: Record<string, {capability: string, outcome: string}> | null}} input
     6	 * @returns {{assignments: Record<string, any>, notes: string[]}}
     7	 */
     8	export function resolveRoles({ config, to, avoid = [], nativeOnly = false, probes = null }) {
     9	  /** @type {string[]} */
    10	  const notes = [];
    11	
    12	  /**
    13	   * The eligibility verdict for a platform, or null when eligible.
    14	   * @param {string} platform @returns {string | null}
    15	   */
    16	  function skipReason(platform) {
    17	    if (avoid.includes(platform)) return 'avoided';
    18	    if (!(platform in (config.platforms ?? {}))) return 'unknown-platform';
    19	    if (config.platforms[platform]?.enabled === false) return 'disabled';
    20	    if (probes && probes[platform]) {
    21	      const { capability, outcome } = probes[platform];
    22	      if (outcome === 'rate-limited') return 'rate-limited';
    23	      if (capability === 'not-installed') return 'not-installed';
    24	      // 'installed' means installation was PROVEN but the probe was capped there
    25	      // (capOnSuccess) — authentication is UNVERIFIED, not verified-failed. Only
    26	      // a probe that ran and did NOT succeed (outcome !== 'ok') is a verified
    27	      // auth/reachability failure worth skipping; a capped success stays
    28	      // selectable and is flagged degraded below (iter-3 F1, plan §Role matrix).
    29	      if (capability === 'installed' && outcome !== 'ok') return 'unauthenticated';
    30	    }
    31	    return null;
    32	  }
    33	
    34	  // A selection is degraded when its availability is UNVERIFIED (gate-2 iter-2
    35	  // M3, iter-3 F1): with no probe cache at all, resolution is offline and EVERY
    36	  // selection is degraded (plan: "Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged
    37	  // degraded"); with a partial cache, platforms the cache is missing are; and a
    38	  // platform probed only to 'installed' (auth unverified, e.g. a --version
    39	  // success) is degraded even though it stays selectable.
    40	  /** @param {string} platform */
    41	  const probeUnverified = (platform) => {
    42	    if (probes == null || !probes[platform]) return true;
    43	    const { capability, outcome } = probes[platform];
    44	    // Degraded when ANY required dimension is unverified: a capped 'installed'
    45	    // (auth never confirmed) OR a non-ok outcome (iter-4 I6) — a selectable
    46	    // platform whose last probe ERRORED or TIMED OUT is presented as
    47	    // reachability-verified otherwise, which is exactly the silent-degradation
    48	    // the plan's degraded flag exists to surface. (rate-limited is skipped, not
    49	    // selected, so it never reaches here.)
    50	    return capability === 'installed' || outcome !== 'ok';
    51	  };
    52	
    53	  /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
    54	  const assignments = {};
    55	  for (const [role, chain] of Object.entries(config.roles)) {
    56	    /** @type {any[]} */
    57	    const skipped = [];
    58	    /** @type {any} */
    59	    let assignment = null;
    60	
    61	    for (let i = 0; i < chain.length; i += 1) {
    62	      const entry = chain[i];
    63	      if (nativeOnly && entry.platform !== to) {
    64	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why: 'non-native' });
    65	        continue;
    66	      }
    67	      const why = skipReason(entry.platform);
    68	      if (why) {
    69	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why });
    70	        continue;
    71	      }
    72	      assignment = {
    73	        platform: entry.platform,
    74	        model: entry.model,
    75	        effort: entry.effort ?? null,
    76	        mode: entry.platform === to ? 'native' : 'delegated',
    77	        chainIndex: i,
    78	        skipped,
    79	      };
    80	      if (probeUnverified(entry.platform)) assignment.degraded = true;
    81	      break;
    82	    }
    83	
    84	    if (!assignment && nativeOnly) {
    85	      // Forced-default is only valid when the destination itself is eligible.
    86	      const whyTo = skipReason(to);
    87	      const model = config.defaults?.[to];
    88	      if (!whyTo && typeof model === 'string') {
    89	        assignment = { platform: to, model, effort: null, mode: 'forced-default', chainIndex: null, skipped };
    90	        if (probeUnverified(to)) assignment.degraded = true;
    91	      }
    92	    }
    93	
    94	    if (!assignment) {
    95	      assignment = { platform: null, model: null, effort: null, mode: 'unavailable', chainIndex: null, skipped };
    96	    }
    97	    assignments[role] = assignment;
    98	  }
    99	
   100	  return { assignments, notes };
   101	}

     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import * as nodeFs from 'node:fs';
     4	import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
     5	import { join, dirname } from 'node:path';
     6	import { classify } from '../../core/src/detect/classifier.mjs';
     7	import { loadSignatures } from '../../core/src/detect/signatures.mjs';
     8	
     9	// The REAL shipped table (fold C1-2): the negative/near-miss corpus is run
    10	// through core/data/signatures.v1.json via the same loader the built-in pin
    11	// tests use, so an overbroad shipped regex that false-positives "usage limit"
    12	// prose is caught here — not just against an inline BASE table.
    13	const here = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
    14	const repoRoot = join(here, '..', '..');
    15	const REAL_SIGNATURES_PATH = join(repoRoot, 'core', 'data', 'signatures.v1.json');
    16	const realIo = { fs: nodeFs };
    17	
    18	// ===========================================================================
    19	// Contract choices for detect/classifier.mjs. Source of truth:
    20	// docs/design/core.md §Module APIs (classify signature + return shape),
    21	// §"signatures/classifier" test list; plan §Core engine — Limit detector
    22	// (structured-first, exit-code rule iteration 3, ANSI strip, precedence,
    23	// reset-hint extraction, negative corpus). classify is PURE.
    24	//
    25	// SIGNATURE (input): classify({text, exitCode, platform, table, structured}).
    26	//   - `table` is the loadSignatures return value; classify reads table.signatures
    27	//     (a FLAT array), and only applies signatures whose `platform` === platform.
    28	//   - Tables here are built inline (plain data) so this file's red phase is
    29	//     attributable to classifier.mjs alone (not signatures.mjs / the data file).
    30	//
    31	// RETURN: { class, signatureId, confidence, resetHint }  (tried? is added by the
    32	//   detect command under --explain, not by classify itself, so it is NOT pinned
    33	//   here). class ∈ 'ok'|'usage-limit'|'auth'|'throttle'|'other-error'.
    34	//
    35	// PINS:
    36	//   1. STRUCTURED-FIRST. TWO structured kinds are recognized, and for BOTH the
    37	//      TEXT AND EXIT CODE ARE IGNORED (structured evidence wins outright):
    38	//        (a) {kind:'stop-failure', errorType} — Claude Code StopFailure enum
    39	//            (platform-notes.md snapshot). errorType maps (fold C1-4 completes
    40	//            the enum — invalid_request / model_not_found / max_output_tokens
    41	//            were previously omitted):
    42	//               rate_limit                         -> usage-limit
    43	//               overloaded                         -> throttle
    44	//               authentication_failed              -> auth
    45	//               oauth_org_not_allowed              -> auth
    46	//               billing_error                      -> auth
    47	//               invalid_request                    -> other-error
    48	//               model_not_found                    -> other-error
    49	//               server_error                       -> other-error
    50	//               max_output_tokens                  -> other-error
    51	//               unknown / any unrecognized value   -> other-error
    52	//        (b) {kind:'exec-json', errorType} — Codex `codex exec --json` limit
    53	//            evidence (fold C1-4). errorType 'usage_limit' -> usage-limit.
    54	//      signatureId = `structured:<errorType>` for both kinds (rate_limit pinned
    55	//      exactly to 'structured:rate_limit'; codex usage_limit -> 'structured:usage_limit').
    56	//      Structured confidence = 'high'. resetHint = null.
    57	//      A structured value with a kind OTHER than these two (e.g. 'some-other-event')
    58	//      is ignored and classification falls through to the text/exit-code tiers.
    59	//   2. TEXT TIER. ANSI escape sequences are stripped before matching.
    60	//      Precedence when multiple signatures match: usage-limit > auth > throttle
    61	//      > other-error. A matched signature's `confidence` and `id` are carried
    62	//      through (signatureId === sig.id, confidence === sig.confidence).
    63	//   3. RESET-HINT EXTRACTION. When the matched signature carries a `resetHint`
    64	//      regex, classify runs it against the (ANSI-stripped) text and returns
    65	//      match[1] ?? match[0] (first capture group if present, else whole match);
    66	//      no regex or no match -> resetHint null. A matched signature with no
    67	//      resetHint -> resetHint null.
    68	//   4. EXIT-CODE RULE. A matching signature WINS regardless of exit code. With
    69	//      NO matching signature: nonzero exit -> other-error (NEVER usage-limit);
    70	//      zero exit -> ok. No-match verdicts carry signatureId null, resetHint null.
    71	//   5. json-field matcher parses each LINE of text as JSON and compares a dotted
    72	//      path to `equals`.
    73	// ===========================================================================
    74	
    75	const ANSI_RED = '\x1b[31m';
    76	const ANSI_RESET = '\x1b[0m';
    77	
    78	const mkTable = (signatures) => ({ schema: 'baton/signatures@1', updated: '2026-07-11', signatures });
    79	const sig = (id, platform, cls, matcher, confidence = 'high', resetHint) => {
    80	  const s = { id, platform, class: cls, confidence, matcher };
    81	  if (resetHint !== undefined) s.resetHint = resetHint;
    82	  return s;
    83	};
    84	
    85	// A small canonical table reused across text-tier tests.
    86	const ccSession = sig('cc/session', 'claude-code', 'usage-limit', { kind: 'substring', value: "You've hit your session limit" }, 'high', 'resets ([^\\n]+)');

    13	 * `baton detect` — classify harness output / structured error types.
    14	 * Exit codes ARE the verdict (frozen contract): 0 ok · 10 usage-limit ·
    15	 * 11 throttle · 12 auth · 13 other-error · 2 usage error.
    16	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    17	 * @returns {number}
    18	 */
    19	export function cmdDetect(args, io) {
    20	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { platform: 'string', text: 'string', 'exit-code': 'string', 'structured-error-type': 'string', signatures: 'string', explain: 'boolean' });
    21	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'detect', parsed.error);
    22	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    23	  const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
    24	  if (!platform) return usageError(io, flags, 'detect', '--platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required');
    25

    35	  } catch (err) {

    38	    else io.stderr.write(`baton detect: ${msg}\n`);
    39	    return 1;
    40	  }

    42	  const text = typeof flags.text === 'string' ? flags.text : typeof io.stdin === 'string' ? io.stdin : '';

     1	const PRECEDENCE = ['usage-limit', 'auth', 'throttle', 'other-error'];
     2	
     3	/** @type {Record<string, string>} */
     4	const STOP_FAILURE_MAP = {
     5	  rate_limit: 'usage-limit',
     6	  overloaded: 'throttle',
     7	  authentication_failed: 'auth',
     8	  oauth_org_not_allowed: 'auth',
     9	  billing_error: 'auth',
    10	};
    11	
    12	// eslint-disable-next-line no-control-regex
    13	const ANSI = /\x1b\[[0-9;]*[A-Za-z]/g;
    14	
    15	/** @param {any} matcher @param {string} text */
    16	function matches(matcher, text) {
    17	  switch (matcher.kind) {
    18	    case 'substring':
    19	      return text.includes(matcher.value);
    20	    case 'regex':
    21	      return new RegExp(matcher.pattern, matcher.flags || '').test(text);
    22	    case 'json-field': {
    23	      for (const line of text.split('\n')) {
    24	        let obj;
    25	        try {
    26	          obj = JSON.parse(line);
    27	        } catch {
    28	          continue;
    29	        }
    30	        const val = String(matcher.path)
    31	          .split('.')
    32	          .reduce((o, k) => (o == null ? undefined : o[k]), obj);
    33	        if (val === matcher.equals) return true;
    34	      }
    35	      return false;
    36	    }
    37	    default:
    38	      return false;
    39	  }
    40	}
    41	
    42	/**
    43	 * Classify harness output. Structured evidence first (text and exit code are
    44	 * ignored when a recognized structured signal is present); text signatures as
    45	 * the fallback tier; exit code only breaks the no-match tie — a generic nonzero
    46	 * exit is never usage-limit on its own.
    47	 * @param {{text?: string, exitCode?: number, platform: string, table: any, structured?: {kind: string, errorType: string} | null}} input
    48	 * @returns {{class: string, signatureId: string | null, confidence: string | null, resetHint: string | null, tried?: string[]}}
    49	 */
    50	export function classify({ text = '', exitCode = 0, platform, table, structured = null }) {
    51	  if (structured) {
    52	    if (structured.kind === 'stop-failure') {
    53	      const cls = STOP_FAILURE_MAP[structured.errorType] ?? 'other-error';
    54	      return { class: cls, signatureId: `structured:${structured.errorType}`, confidence: 'high', resetHint: null };
    55	    }
    56	    if (structured.kind === 'exec-json') {
    57	      const cls = structured.errorType === 'usage_limit' ? 'usage-limit' : 'other-error';
    58	      return { class: cls, signatureId: `structured:${structured.errorType}`, confidence: 'high', resetHint: null };
    59	    }
    60	    // Unrecognized structured kind: fall through to the text tier.
    61	  }
    62	
    63	  const stripped = text.replace(ANSI, '');
    64	  const candidates = table.signatures.filter((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.platform === platform);
    65	  /** @type {any} */
    66	  let best = null;
    67	  for (const s of candidates) {
    68	    if (!matches(s.matcher, stripped)) continue;
    69	    if (best === null || PRECEDENCE.indexOf(s.class) < PRECEDENCE.indexOf(best.class)) best = s;
    70	  }
    71	
    72	  if (best) {
    73	    let resetHint = null;
    74	    if (typeof best.resetHint === 'string') {
    75	      const m = stripped.match(new RegExp(best.resetHint));
    76	      if (m) resetHint = m[1] ?? m[0];
    77	    }
    78	    return { class: best.class, signatureId: best.id, confidence: best.confidence, resetHint };
    79	  }
    80	
    81	  return {
    82	    class: exitCode !== 0 ? 'other-error' : 'ok',
    83	    signatureId: null,
    84	    confidence: null,
    85	    resetHint: null,
    86	  };
    87	}

     5	const MATCHER_KINDS = ['substring', 'regex', 'json-field'];
     6	const REGEX_MAX_LEN = 200;
     7	
     8	/**
     9	 * Is a quantified group's body ambiguous? Alternation, another quantifier, or
    10	 * any nested group inside a `(…)+`/`(…)*`/`(…){` makes backtracking
    11	 * super-linear in the worst case — conservatively rejected (gate-2 fix 6:
    12	 * the old lint accepted `(a|aa)+$` and the `(a|a)*x` live repro).
    13	 * @param {string} body
    14	 */
    15	function riskyGroupBody(body) {
    16	  // Strip a non-capture/lookaround/named marker so `(?:abc)+` reads as `abc`.
    17	  const b = body.replace(/^\?(?:<=|<!|:|=|!|<[^>]*>)/, '');
    18	  let inClass = false;
    19	  for (let i = 0; i < b.length; i++) {
    20	    const c = b[i];
    21	    if (c === '\\') {
    22	      i++;
    23	      continue;
    24	    }
    25	    if (inClass) {
    26	      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
    27	      continue;
    28	    }
    29	    if (c === '[') {
    30	      inClass = true;
    31	      continue;
    32	    }
    33	    if (c === '|' || c === '+' || c === '*' || c === '{' || c === '?' || c === '(' || c === ')') return true;
    34	  }
    35	  return false;
    36	}
    37	
    38	/**
    39	 * Structural scan: walk the pattern (class- and escape-aware), and for every
    40	 * group followed by an unbounded quantifier check its full body — any nesting
    41	 * depth — for ambiguity. Returns a problem string or null.
    42	 * @param {string} pattern
    43	 */
    44	function scanQuantifiedGroups(pattern) {
    45	  /** @type {number[]} */
    46	  const opens = [];
    47	  let inClass = false;
    48	  for (let i = 0; i < pattern.length; i++) {
    49	    const c = pattern[i];
    50	    if (c === '\\') {
    51	      i++;
    52	      continue;
    53	    }
    54	    if (inClass) {
    55	      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
    56	      continue;
    57	    }
    58	    if (c === '[') {
    59	      inClass = true;
    60	      continue;
    61	    }
    62	    if (c === '(') {
    63	      opens.push(i);
    64	      continue;
    65	    }
    66	    if (c === ')') {
    67	      const start = opens.pop();
    68	      if (start === undefined) continue; // unbalanced — new RegExp() reports it
    69	      const next = pattern[i + 1];
    70	      if (next === '+' || next === '*' || next === '{') {
    71	        const body = pattern.slice(start + 1, i);
    72	        if (riskyGroupBody(body)) {
    73	          return `quantified group "(${body})" contains alternation, another quantifier, or a nested group`;
    74	        }
    75	      }
    76	    }
    77	  }
    78	  return null;
    79	}
